# Equity Bridge, EV and Invested Capital Count Every Claim Once Implementation Plan (B4a and B4b)

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (- [ ]) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Make the DCF equity bridge, the market and REIT enterprise values, invested capital (ROIC, sales-to-capital and its filed history) and the per-share divisors count every claim on the business exactly once, on one lease basis, fixing the 21 B4 findings of the 2026-10-27 audit, and VAL-14 from the claim audit of AMZN report #52 (F-04; B4b, pre-flight addendum).

**Two parts, two PRs (pre-flight split).** One plan file holds both.
- **Part A, B4a (PR 1, next after B13):** Tasks 1-7, 9, 12 and 13, then B4a's batch tasks 14-24. It carries FIN-103, FIN-119, FIN-119-V1, FIN-119-V2, FIN-303, FIN-303-V1, FIN-108, FIN-304, FIN-305, FIN-305-V1, FIN-305-V2, FIN-403, FIN-404, FIN-109 and FIN-311, three of them High (FIN-119, FIN-303, FIN-305). B4a changes Stage B and the display chain; it changes no statements-builder rule.
- **Part B, B4b (PR 2, cut after B4a merges):** Task 26 (bases, no commit), Tasks 8, 25 (VAL-14, new), 10 and 11, then B4b's batch tasks 14b-24b, each written as a delta on its B4a counterpart. It carries FIN-401, FIN-401-V1, FIN-302, FIN-302-V1, FIN-302-V2, FIN-407 and VAL-14, three of them High (FIN-401, FIN-302, FIN-302-V1). Tasks 8 and 11 are redesigned (pre-flight C1, C2, I4, I5). B4b edits the statements builder, so B13's rebuild harness is mandatory for its replay. It has its own stamps, since-version, D-number and replay.
- Moved tasks keep their numbers, and their old places carry a stub. B6 Task 5 follows B4b.

**Code references.** `file:line` references are at `main` `ca3726b` for the files B13 does not touch (`compute.ts`, `valuation.ts`, `returns.ts`, `projections.ts`, `debtLikeObligations.ts`, `fairValue.ts`, `auditTables.ts`, `scenarioPresentation.ts`, `fmp.ts`, `stageA/validate.ts`; checked with `git diff --quiet ca3726b 668c294`), and at B13's `668c294` (`fix/edgar-derived-lines`, worktree `tmp/fixes/top15-spend`) for the files B13 edits (`statements.ts`, `tagSynonyms.ts`, `netDebt.ts`, `financialEnrichment.ts`, `synonymFloor.ts`, `presentationLineage.ts`, the five wrapper sites, the stamp files, `docs/METHODOLOGY.md`). **They are re-taken at B13's merge commit before any task is dispatched**, and each edit is re-located by its quoted code (pre-flight minor; the review's stale-reference table, section 1).

**Architecture:** One balance row feeds the DCF bridge (the statement-date row D-65 already promises), and three shared predicates replace today's per-consumer copies: `temporaryEquityClaims` (redeemable preferred and redeemable NCI, new `src/pipeline/stageB/seniorClaims.ts`), `operatingLeaseOutOfDebt` (the CODE-1-proven lease slice, `src/pipeline/stageB/netDebt.ts`) and `oneReadingPerConvertible` (claim or converted, never both, new `src/pipeline/stageB/convertibles.ts`). Every consumer (DCF bridge, multiples EV, own-history EV, REIT block, ROIC, sales-to-capital and its A-2 history, excess-return per share, forecast EPS) reads them, and the bridge result (`DcfResult.bridge`, D-26) carries the chosen reading so every re-run inherits it. The statements builder states where equity-method income sits, and the E-7 debt-like-obligation reader gains three tag families; every debt-like line is deducted after tax at the D-98 marginal rate (decisions spec 4.3). Stored reports keep their bytes (D-67); one display-time rule marks legacy DCF and excess-return values computed before this batch, each clause under its own part's frozen since-version. B4a builds `temporaryEquityClaims`, `operatingLeaseOutOfDebt`, the after-tax debt-like lines and the loss-period divisor; B4b adds `oneReadingPerConvertible`, the equity-method placement and VAL-14's fair-value financial assets.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript on the Next.js 16 app (no task in this batch modifies `src/app`, route handlers or `src/proxy.ts`; if an implementer finds one must, read `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/01-getting-started/05-server-and-client-components.md` and `06-fetching-data.md` first), Vitest (hermetic: `tests/setup` blocks live network), the synthetic Stage B harness (`tests/helpers/currencyBundle.ts`, direct `valueCompany` fixtures as in `tests/stageB.debtLikeObligations.audit20261022.test.ts`), inline trimmed SEC companyfacts, `npm run audit:deltas`, `npm run research:replay`.

**Spec:** `tmp/audit-2026-10-27/REPORT.md` (section 7, batch B4), `tmp/audit-2026-10-27/findings.json`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-27-audit-remediation-decisions.md` (section 2.4 for this batch's decisions; section 4.3, "Main-session resolutions (2026-10-28)", is binding and is applied in this plan, see "Integration notes (2026-10-28)"). Where the spec's section 2.4 summary and this plan differ, this plan is the authority, except for the 4.3 resolutions, which this plan now follows. The pre-flight review (`.superpowers/sdd/2026-10-27-audit-fix-04-equity-bridge-and-invested-capital/preflight-review.md`) and the controller's rulings on it (`preflight-rulings.md` in the same folder, binding, with its addendum) are applied in "Pre-flight amendments (2026-10-29)" below.

**Branches.**
- **B4a:** `fix/equity-bridge-and-invested-capital`, cut from an up-to-date `main` after B13 has merged (`git fetch origin && git switch -c fix/equity-bridge-and-invested-capital origin/main`). Merge order (master plan `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-27-audit-remediation.md`, queue slots 4 and 5): B3, then B13, then B4a. B13 changes the derived-EBIT operands, the `first` chain step (`unlessBelow`) and the stored-report view chain that Tasks 8, 15 and 21 build on.
- **B4b:** `fix/equity-bridge-readings`, cut from `main` after B4a merges (Task 26).
- B5 and B6 rebase on B4 (they edit `valuation.ts`, `compute.ts`, `returns.ts` and `projections.ts` too) and take B4's signatures. B6 Task 5 (`perShareOf`) follows B4b, because it is written against Task 11's excess-return divisor. R1 (R1a, R1b) runs after B4, B5, B2b and B6 (pre-flight I11; Global Constraints, cross-batch).
- Never use a junction to shared data: copy harnesses and inputs (worktree-junction rule).

## Global Constraints

- Failing test first for every finding; if a finding cannot be made to fail at HEAD, skip it and say so in the PR. For all 21 findings, the code they cite read as the findings describe at HEAD 6542531, where it was re-read line by line for this plan. `main` has since moved 204 commits (R0 rewrote the lease-scope texts with amounts in millions, the ROIC refusal channel and the stakes fill); the pre-flight review spot-checked every cited defect at `ca3726b` and each still holds (FIN-103 text, FIN-108's REIT formula `valuation.ts:5608-5614`, FIN-109 text, FIN-303 and FIN-303-V1, FIN-304, FIN-401 note). The probes were not re-run, because they rewrite evidence logs. Each task's Step 2 is therefore the first fresh confirmation, and a test that unexpectedly passes there means the finding is skipped and reported.
- Smallest change that fixes the whole class; never ticker-specific code (no CIK, symbol or company name in `src/`); no unrelated refactoring.
- One commit per task; stage files by name (`git add <path> ...`), never `git add -A` or `git add .` (tmp/, the brag-output folders and `docs/independent-audit-2026-10-19/` must stay uncommitted).
- Tests are hermetic: no live network, no FMP/SEC/Yahoo/FRED/AI calls, no paid calls, never a report for a real ticker. Fixtures are synthetic or inline trimmed SEC companyfacts (public SEC data, allowed by `docs/DATA-RIGHTS.md`; amounts copied from the filings the findings cite). Never commit FMP rows, run-input snapshots or anything else from `tmp/`.
- The probes in `tmp/audit-2026-10-27/probes/` are seeds only; do not run them (several rewrite evidence logs). Copy their logic into `tests/`, drop every `fs` read of `tmp/` or `docs/independent-audit-2026-10-19/` and every `fs.writeFileSync`.
- Every behaviour change updates `docs/METHODOLOGY.md` and any other markdown describing it (`README.md`, `docs/RESEARCH.md`, `docs/superpowers/specs/*`) in the same commit; grep the docs for the old wording before committing.
- Version stamps move once per part, in Task 16 (B4a) and Task 16b (B4b): `STAGE_B_IMPLEMENTATION_VERSION` (`src/pipeline/runInputSnapshot.ts:33` at B13, there `STAGE_B_2026_10_ACCOUNTING_EVIDENCE_30`), `PAYLOAD_VERSION` (`src/pipeline/stageC/payload.ts:433`, there `1.51.0`), `REPORT_SPEC_VERSION` (`src/report/schema.ts:144`, there `1.49.0`); `tests/report.versionStamps.test.ts` moves with them. `PIPELINE_VERSION` and `SCORE_BANDS_VERSION` do not move (no Stage C pass logic or band table changes).
- **Numbering at merge (pre-flight I8).** Every stamp, since-version and D-number is "next after the preceding merge", and every stamp line, history comment, CHANGELOG line and DECISIONS heading says so; write "D-next" until merge. The values below are the expected ones, re-taken at execution:

## Owner decisions

The owner delegated these calls ("Use your recommendation based on research to make this a professional accurate repository"). They are recorded one "D-next" entry per part, one bullet per decision (the repository's one-entry-per-batch practice, D-110 to D-116): B4a's entry (Task 20) holds OD-1, OD-3, OD-4, OD-6, OD-7 and OD-8, and B4b's (Task 20b) holds OD-2, OD-5 and OD-9, with OD-1's convertible-preferred extension (C2).

### OD-1. Redeemable (temporary) equity: a claim in the bridge, the market EV and invested capital (FIN-119, FIN-119-V1, FIN-119-V2 mezzanine half)

- **Question.** Is redeemable NCI deducted at its carrying (redemption) value as a floor inside the E-5 test, or only disclosed? Does redeemable preferred with an unknown parent/NCI split withhold the bridge? Does mezzanine capital enter invested capital at carrying value, and what does a refused allocation do?
- **Decision.** One house definition of claims senior to common: permanent preferred + redeemable preferred (`TemporaryEquityCarryingAmountAttributableToParent`), and book NCI + redeemable NCI (`RedeemableNoncontrollingInterestEquityCarryingAmount`).
  - The DCF bridge deducts redeemable preferred beside preferred. Redeemable NCI joins book NCI as the book floor of the E-5 `max(book, profit share x consolidated equity value)` test, so it is never deducted twice.
  - **A null book NCI is not a zero book (pre-flight I2).** A null book NCI never becomes 0 plus the redeemable NCI. When book NCI is null and a redeemable NCI is filed, the combined NCI claim stays withheld as E-5 says ("a null book is not a zero claim"): the per share is withheld, and the reason names the redeemable NCI and its amount. The redeemable NCI is itself evidence of outside holders, as signed NCI income is in E-5.
  - The market EV, the own-history EV and the REIT EV add both, like their permanent counterparts.
  - Invested capital (ROIC, DCF sales-to-capital, A-2 history) adds the admitted temporary-equity total: it is financing capital, and the cash it raised is already netted. A refused temporary-equity year joins `returns.roic`'s existing refusal list (R0, D-133) with the non-debt kind `"temporary-equity"`, so it never turns R0's WACC-balance critical rule on (pre-flight I3).
  - **B4b extension (pre-flight C2).** The claim set also holds the filed convertible-preferred carrying value (OD-2), so every filed preferred claim, convertible or not, is deducted exactly once.
  - **Measured at main (pre-flight I1).** Temporary equity is present at `main` within the latest four quarters or three years: UNH 1.436B at its 2026-06-30 bridge date (1.608B, 4.323B and 4.498B for FY2025, FY2024 and FY2023), WMT 0.293B at 2026-07-31, T 2.006B at 2026-09-30, CMCSA 0.185B at 2026-06-30 and TSLA 0.054B (total) at 2026-06-30, all redeemable NCI; in history years only, NEE (FY2024 0.401B, FY2023 1.256B), DIS (FY2023 9.055B) and CVX (FY2023 0.166B). UNH and WMT publish DCFs, with the E-5 basis "book" for both, so they are expected movers in the right direction: UNH's DCF ≈ −$1.56 (−0.4%) and its ROIC 11.15% → ≈10.93%; WMT ≈ −$0.04 (−0.09%). The market and own-history EVs add the claim for UNH, WMT and TSLA (the REIT block is not affected). Forecast EPS and P/FCF are already withheld for UNH and WMT by their book NCI, so nothing new is withheld. The replay re-measures every figure.
  - A total with no parent/NCI split is deducted beside preferred. If the E-5 earnings-share basis would apply in the same bridge, the per-share value is withheld (`valuation.dcf.temporaryEquityAllocation`, warn), because the unsplit amount may be NCI the earnings key already sizes.
  - An operand Stage A refuses (`fieldConflicts.usable === false`, non-finite, total vs parts conflict) withholds what it touches: the bridge per share, the EV multiples and that year's ROIC. The operand is never zero-filled (D-87: missing components remain unknown).
  - The same claims reach every guard that today asks whether preferred or NCI claims exist: forecast operating EPS (`projections.ts:685-690` withholds when `bridge.preferred` or `bridge.minorityInterest` is non-zero, so an issuer with temporary equity now has its forecast EPS withheld exactly as one with permanent preferred), the P/FCF cash-ownership guard (`valuation.ts:3785-3790`) and the own-history common-allocation checks (`valuation.ts:4399`, `:4426`). One definition of "claims senior to common", applied everywhere.
  - The parent part is labelled "temporary equity attributable to the parent (redeemable preferred and other redeemable parent equity)", not "redeemable preferred": `TemporaryEquityCarryingAmountAttributableToParent` can also hold redeemable common shares (ESOP puts, SPAC Class A shares subject to redemption) that the diluted count already holds. Companyfacts do not separate the two, so that case is a disclosed residual (decisions spec 4.3, B4): the deducted amount stays in the bridge, and the bridge note says in the report itself that the caption can hold redeemable common shares the diluted count may already include, which would then be counted twice (Task 2 test "the parent temporary-equity note discloses the redeemable-common residual"). The disposition note records it (Task 19). It is never silently assumed away.
- **Alternatives rejected.** Disclose only: it leaves a known, sourced claim inside common equity, and the bridge itemises E-7 lines as if complete. Deduct redeemable NCI outside E-5: it double counts whenever the earnings key applies. Zero-fill a refused allocation: D-87 forbids it.
- **Revises.** D-87's scope note ("do not ... alter valuation claims without a separate admission basis", `docs/audit/2026-10-18-accounting-evidence-remediation.md:7`); this entry is that admission basis. Extends E-5 (D-102) with the redeemable floor.
- **Sources.**
  - Deloitte Roadmap, *Noncontrolling Interests* 9.4 (https://dart.deloitte.com/USDART/home/codification/broad-transactions/asc810-10/roadmap-noncontrolling-interests/chapter-9-redeemable-noncontrolling-interests/9-4-accounting-for-redeemable-noncontrolling), checked 2026-10-27: 9.4.1 quotes ASR 268 ("preferred securities that are redeemable for cash or other assets to be classified outside of permanent equity") and extends it to "common-share and preferred-share redeemable noncontrolling interests"; 9.4.3 says a currently redeemable interest "should be adjusted to its maximum redemption amount at the balance sheet date". The carrying amount is therefore the redemption claim.
  - Deloitte Roadmap, *SEC Comment Letter Considerations* 2.16 (https://dart.deloitte.com/USDART/home/publications/deloitte/additional-deloitte-guidance/roadmap-sec-comment-letter-considerations/chapter-2-financial-statement-accounting-disclosure/2-16-noncontrolling-interests): "redeemable equity classified outside permanent equity should not be included in any total or subtotal captioned 'total equity'". This is why no equity field the bridge reads carries the amount (`tagSynonyms.ts:547-549` at B13).
  - Koller, Goedhart and Wessels, *Valuation* 7th ed., ch. 16 "Moving from Enterprise Value to Value per Share" (https://www.oreilly.com/library/view/valuation-7th-edition/9781119610885/c16.xhtml): equity value is enterprise value less every nonequity claim, hybrid securities and noncontrolling interests among them.

### OD-2. Convertibles: one reading per instrument, decided once for both routes (FIN-302, FIN-302-V1, FIN-302-V2; B4b)

- **Question.** Which reading does the bridge publish for a convertible whose if-converted shares the filed diluted count already holds? The options are lower of claim and converted, always claim, or always converted. Is the choice made once for the DCF and the excess-return route?
- **Decision.** One reading per instrument, chosen by moneyness at the model's own value, identically on the general and financial routes.
  - The reader takes the filed if-converted increment N (`IncrementalCommonSharesAttributableToConversionOfDebtSecurities`, `...ConversionOfPreferredStock`) for the divisor row's exact period, and the instrument's claim C at the bridge date.
  - **Claim reading:** C stays deducted and N leaves the divisor. **Converted reading:** C is restored to equity and N stays in the divisor.
  - The converted reading is lower exactly when C/N (the effective conversion price) is below the claim reading's per-share value, i.e. when the instrument is in the money at the value the model assigns. The bridge publishes the lower of the two readings: in the money means shares, out of the money means a claim.
  - With two instruments, all four combinations are compared and the lowest is kept.
  - The chosen reading lives on `DcfResult.bridge.convertibles` and in the divisor and claims every re-run reads (D-26). Forecast operating EPS follows it: the claim reading divides by D − N, and the converted reading for notes drops the note's interest at the run-rate's rate.
  - When N is filed but C cannot be read at the bridge date, main's figure stays (main's claim and the filed divisor) with a warn gap naming the instrument. That gap bounds the understatement at N/(D − N). When convertible debt or preferred is tagged but no increment is filed for the period, an info gap says the divisor may hold conversion shares.
  - **Every filed preferred claim is deducted exactly once (pre-flight C2).** At `main` the bridge deducts no convertible preferred: the `preferredStock` synonyms (`tagSynonyms.ts:534-537` at B13) read only `PreferredStockIncludingAdditionalPaidInCapital…` and `PreferredStockValue`. GOOGL files `ConvertiblePreferredStockNonredeemableOrRedeemableIssuerOptionValue` 18.023B at 2026-06-30 (0 at 2026-03-31: a series issued in Q2 2026) and an increment of 16,000,000 shares for the quarter (8,000,000 for the six months), while its row's `preferredStock` is null. Reading C against a bridge that never deducted it would raise GOOGL above main (+0.13% on the claim reading, +0.68% converted), so:
    - the bridge deducts every filed preferred claim, convertible or not, at the bridge date, including a series issued in the period: the filed convertible-preferred carrying value joins OD-1's claim set beside permanent and redeemable preferred. Where the row's `preferredStock` is also positive at that date (addendum 2, item 1): when the filing distinguishes the series (separate series tags, or series named in the notes), each series is deducted once; when it cannot tell whether one holds the other, the larger is deducted (never the sum, which would count an overlapping series twice, and never less than main), with the warn gap `valuation.dcf.preferredClaimOverlap` naming both amounts. Task 11 Step 0 counts how often the census files both, and B4b's pre-flight checks this rule against GOOGL's actual tags before any code;
    - the converted reading never restores more than the bridge deducted for that instrument (`restored.preferred` ≤ the amount deducted for the series; for notes, the claim counts as deducted only when the notes are inside the bridge's total debt, D-95);
    - where the claim cannot be established (no filed carrying value of the instrument at the bridge date), main's figure stays, with the warn gap `valuation.perShare.convertibleClaim` naming the instrument. The earlier fallback to the bridge's whole preferred deduction is removed: that deduction may not hold the series;
    - a series issued in the period carries a part-period increment (GOOGL's 16M is a quarter's weighting, not its full conversion count). With its claim deducted, its converted reading (E − C + C)/D is main's own figure exactly, never above it, and the claim reading (E − C)/(D − N) is the lower one;
    - expected: GOOGL ≈ −0.55% (215.425 → ≈214.240 on main's census row, (E − C)/(D − N)), the real claim deducted; Task 21b measures it. A deducted convertible-preferred claim reaches the forecast-EPS ownership guard (`projections.ts:685-690`) like any preferred, so on the claim reading the issuer's forecast EPS is withheld (OD-1's one definition of claims senior to common); Task 21b reports GOOGL's.
  - **Notes that settle their principal in cash (review addition).** Under ASC 260-10-45-40(b) as amended by ASU 2020-06, a note whose terms require the principal to be paid in cash adds only the conversion-spread shares to the diluted count, and its principal is a claim. For such a note HEAD's figure (claim deducted, spread shares in the divisor) is already the consistent one, and either B4 reading would overstate value (the claim reading drops the spread shares; the converted reading restores the principal without the full conversion shares). The two readings are therefore offered only for an instrument whose filed N is shown to be its FULL conversion share count:
    - convertible preferred: always (the if-converted method counts the full shares; ASU 2020-06 does not let the issuer rebut share settlement);
    - notes: only when an undimensioned `DebtInstrumentConvertibleConversionPrice1` K is filed at or within a year before the bridge date (by `observedAt`) and |N − C/K| ≤ 5% of C/K (house tolerance: carrying amount vs principal differs by issuance cost only). N below 95% of C/K is read as spread-only ("net-share": claim kept, N kept, info note naming ASC 260-10-45-40(b)). No K filed: "unresolved" (claim kept, N kept: HEAD's figure, the lowest) with the warn gap `valuation.perShare.convertibleClaim` bounding the understatement at N/(D − N).
      - **No step at the 95% and 105% edges (addendum 2, item 6; M6).** As first written, a note with N at 94.9% of C/K reads spread-only and one at 95.0% takes the two readings, so the per share jumps at each edge of the band. The thresholds must not make a step: B4b blends the two readings linearly in N/(C/K) across the band, or adopts one continuous rule. B4b's pre-flight picks which, and Task 11 tests continuity at both edges: the per share just inside and just outside each edge differs only by the size of the step in N. No census issuer files K within a year (Task 11 Step 0), so no stored figure moves.
  - **How often K is filed (measured, decisions spec 4.3, B4).** Task 11 Step 0 measures it offline over the 45 census snapshots' own companyfacts (`tmp/metis-audit-2026-10-22/scratch/snapshots/main/*.json`, `bundle.edgar.companyFacts.value.data`; no SEC call) before the note wording is fixed. The integration pass ran the same read-only count on 2026-10-28: no census issuer files an undimensioned K within 366 days of its latest balance date (three filed one before 2020: NVDA, last period 2019-01-27; PLD, 2014-06-30; TSLA, 2014-03-31; TSM files no us-gaap `Assets` and is out of scope). Two issuers file a convertible-debt increment in the latest year (DUK, 1,000,000 shares for 2026-01-01..2026-03-31; EXPE, 3,410,000 for FY2025 and 0 for its latest quarter), and both lack K. Two file a convertible-preferred increment (GOOGL, 16,000,000 for the quarter to 2026-06-30, with `ConvertiblePreferredStockNonredeemableOrRedeemableIssuerOptionValue` 18,023,000,000; PG, 68,300,000 for FY2026). So a convertible note almost always reads "unresolved" (HEAD's lower figure with the bound), and the two readings land mainly for convertible preferred, where C2's deduction of the filed claim comes first. The METHODOLOGY sentence, the disposition note and the PR say exactly that (wording W-rare in Task 11 Step 0). If Step 0 re-measures differently at branch time, its decision rule picks the wording.
- **Why the model's value and not the quote.** The brief's default says "in the money", and this keeps it, measured where the per-share value is formed. Tested at the quote, a stock the model values below the conversion price would publish the higher, converted reading, and the intrinsic value would depend on the market price it is compared with.
- **Alternatives rejected.** Always claim: it overstates value when deeply in the money (the claim at carrying value is worth less than the N shares holders will take). Always converted: it overstates value out of the money (C restored, but holders will take cash). Option-pricing split (Damodaran): no market price or volatility input in the run; recorded as a known approximation (time value ignored).
- **Revises.** D-60 and D-65 item 5 (the diluted divisor's stated reasoning covers options and RSUs only); D-95 (convertible carrying debt treated as debt, silent on conversion shares).
- **Sources.**
  - Damodaran, "Dealing with Hybrids" (https://aswathdamodaran.substack.com/p/dealing-with-hybrids-09-03-19): "Add the debt portion to the rest of the debt, the conversion option value to equity". Each piece is counted once, on one side.
  - Damodaran, "Dealing with Warrants, Convertibles and other Equity Securities in Valuation" (https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~adamodar/New_Home_Page/lectures/eqshare.htm), step 4: "Divide the remaining value of equity by the number of shares outstanding to get value per share". The conversion option is subtracted as an equity claim in step 3 and the divisor holds shares outstanding only, so nothing is counted twice.
  - Equity Methods, "Diluted EPS for Convertible Debt Securities: A Primer" (https://equitymethods.com/articles/diluted-eps-for-convertible-debt-securities-a-primer/): the if-converted method is "the prescribed approach for convertible debt". (The page does not itself name ASU 2020-06; the amendment that removed the treasury-stock alternative and requires presumed share settlement is ASU 2020-06 / ASC 260-10-45-40, see KPMG's Hot Topic "ASU 2020-06 convertible debt", https://kpmg.com/kpmg-us/content/dam/kpmg/frv/pdf/2020/hot-topic-asu-2020-06-convertible-debt.pdf; that PDF could not be text-extracted during review, so the implementer quotes the codification paragraph from it or from the FASB ASU before citing it in DECISIONS.) This is why the filed diluted count holds N for any dilutive share-settled convertible.
  - IB Interview Questions, "Convertible Debt Accounting" (https://ibinterviewquestions.com/guides/accounting-investment-banking/convertible-debt-accounting), practitioner summary of ASU 2020-06: "When the terms require the principal to be paid in cash, only the conversion spread" is settled in shares, "so only the shares needed for that spread are added and no interest is added back". This is the cash-principal rule above.
  - IB Interview Questions, "Convertible Securities, Warrants: Valuation and Dilution" (https://ibinterviewquestions.com/guides/valuation-investment-banking/convertible-securities-warrants-valuation-dilution), checked 2026-10-27, practitioner convention: "in-the-money convertibles are treated as equity (shares added to diluted count, instrument removed from EV bridge debt)", "out-of-the-money convertibles remain as debt or preferred in the bridge", and "never include a convertible both as debt in the bridge and as dilutive shares in the share count".

### OD-3. Lease basis for invested capital: extend D-25 to sales-to-capital with the CODE-1 proof (FIN-303, FIN-303-V1, FIN-108)

- **Question.** Does the DCF's sales-to-capital (current balance and A-2 history) extend D-25's lease-excluded default, or keep a lease-inclusive basis on purpose?
- **Decision.** Extend D-25. On the default switch (`METIS_EV_INCLUDE_LEASES` unset), every invested-capital consumer removes the operating-lease slice from total debt exactly when the bridge does: the slice is positive, at most total debt, and `operatingLeaseScopeUnproven(row) === null` (the CODE-1 proof, D-97 item 3). The consumers are DCF sales-to-capital, the A-2 history row by row, ROIC, the REIT EV and the WACC debt leg (which already does this).
  - With the switch set, every consumer keeps the slice.
  - An unproven row keeps total debt as reported everywhere, and its note says the scope is unproven.
  - One shared predicate, `operatingLeaseOutOfDebt`, replaces the per-consumer copies.
  - **One lease basis per A-2 median (pre-flight minor).** The A-2 history is measured on the current balance's lease basis only. On the lease-excluded basis, a history year whose scope is unproven cannot be put on that basis, so it is left out of the median with a note, as the FCX-1 mode already skips a row it cannot measure; it is never kept lease-inclusive beside lease-excluded years in one median. On a lease-inclusive basis (switch set, or the current row unproven) every year keeps its lease.
  - **Lease-emptied capital (pre-flight I6).** Once the lease leaves debt, invested capital on the consistent lease basis can be ≤ 0 for a negative-equity lessee (restaurant or retail shapes), which at `main` meets the critical `ic <= 0` gap (`valuation.ts:2135-2143`) before Task 5's cap. Then:
    - sales-to-capital uses the company's own established history on the same basis (`establishedSalesToCapital` with the same lease object), when at least three usable years exist (A-2's own minimum, `S2C_HISTORY_MIN_YEARS`, and its ratio rules), within the house cap;
    - otherwise the DCF is withheld (the critical `valuation.dcf.salesToCapital` gap), with the reason naming invested capital and the lease basis.
    - It never falls back to the mixed (lease-inclusive) basis. No census issuer is affected (no DCF went null in the pre-flight emulation).
- **Alternatives rejected.** A lease-inclusive sales-to-capital on purpose: it charges leased capacity twice (its cost is inside the projected EBIT, and its liability is in the capital that reinvestment must rebuild). D-25 rejected that mix because it leaves the rate, the return and the EV "on three different bases". Capitalising leases everywhere (D-25 option b): a larger model change with an imputed interest figure, already rejected in D-25.
- **Revises.** Nothing: it applies D-25 and D-97 item 3 to two omitted consumers. It replaces the batch2b review row (`docs/audit/2026-10-22-audit-batch2b-valuation-mediums.md:49`) that treated leases as capital for the A-2 history without a D-entry.
- **Sources.**
  - CBIZ, "What changes to the lease accounting standards means for business valuation" (https://www.cbiz.com/insights/article/what-changes-to-the-lease-accounting-standards-means-for-business-valuation): the ASC 842 operating-lease expense stays above EBITDA. If leases are treated as debt, "EBITDA should be computed by adding back related operating lease rent to arrive at EBITDAR"; mixing the two means "value could be diminished because of a reduction in cash flows and subtraction of the operating lease debt" (checked 2026-10-27).
  - D-25 itself (`docs/audit/DECISIONS.md:1809-1815`): under ASC 842 NOPAT is already after operating-lease cost, so a lease-inclusive denominator mismeasures capital.

### OD-4. Debt-like obligations beyond E-7: pension and OPEB deficits, litigation accruals, contingent consideration (FIN-305, FIN-305-V1, FIN-305-V2)

- **Question.** Do pension and OPEB deficits, litigation and other loss-contingency accruals, and business-combination earn-outs join E-7, or are they only disclosed? If they join: before or after tax, with what offsets, and is postemployment counted?
- **Decision.** All three join E-7's reader and bridge lines under E-7's conventions, except the tax convention, which changes for all five families (decisions spec 4.3, B4, binding):
  - read at the bridge date, else the latest filed balance within a year (no further back than an annual balance sheet that no longer tags it);
  - a balance filed as zero ends the look-back;
  - **deducted after tax, at the D-98 marginal rate, for all five debt-like families** (asset-retirement obligations, environmental accruals, pension and OPEB deficits, loss-contingency accruals, contingent consideration). This revises E-7's before-tax convention (D-104) and resolves known issue B2b-1 (the batch-2b "not fixed" line, `docs/audit/2026-10-22-audit-batch2b-valuation-mediums.md:55`: "A tax-effected deduction needs the deferred-tax asset on each obligation, which issuers do not tag consistently").
    - The rate is the one the bridge already uses to tax the investments it adds: `assumptions.investmentTax.ratePct`, the marginal-rate floor of D-58 and D-98 (`marginalTaxFloor(domicileCountry)`: 21% for a US domicile, the 26 U.S.C. §11(b) federal rate with state taxes excluded; the 15% global minimum elsewhere). It is not the filer's deferred-tax asset, which is not tagged per obligation; one marginal rate on both sides of the bridge keeps one tax convention per bridge.
    - Each line deducts `net × (1 − rate)`, where `net` is the amount E-7 deducts today (an asset-retirement obligation after its settlement-fund offset). The relief is applied to the amount deducted, never to the offset.
    - The line label says "(filed, after tax at {rate}%…)", and the closing note says the relief is approximate: the rate omits state and local taxes, it assumes the deduction is taken against taxable income in the year the claim is paid, and a payment that is not deductible (a government fine or penalty, 26 U.S.C. §162(f)) or that only adds to tax basis (contingent consideration, which adds to the basis of the acquired assets, amortised over 15 years for intangibles, or of the acquired shares, recovered only on a sale) is credited a relief it will not earn, or not for years, so that claim is understated by up to rate × amount.
    - For a pension deficit, `net × (1 − rate)` is the after-tax cost of funding the deficit at once: the deficit is discounted at a pretax high-quality bond rate (ASC 715), the contribution is deductible, and the fund then earns that rate untaxed. Feldstein's worked example is exactly this case (a $32.20 contribution "would be only $16.10" net at a 50% rate, 1982, p. 78). A deficit funded later costs more in present value on his assumptions; the bridge does not model the funding schedule, and the disposition note records it.
    - If no marginal rate is resolved (`assumptions.investmentTax` absent or not positive, which `buildDcfAssumptions` never produces today), the line is deducted at its full amount and the note keeps E-7's before-tax sentence: the conservative side, never a silent zero relief.
  - **Pension and OPEB:** the filer's broadest recognised liability family at the date, never summed across families, in this order: (a) `PensionAndOtherPostretirementAndPostemploymentBenefitPlansLiabilities{Noncurrent,Current}`, (b) `PensionAndOtherPostretirementDefinedBenefitPlansLiabilities{Noncurrent,Current}`, (c) `DefinedBenefitPensionPlanLiabilities{Noncurrent,Current}` plus `OtherPostretirementDefinedBenefitPlanLiabilities{Noncurrent,Current}`. Overfunded-plan assets are not credited. Postemployment benefits (ASC 712) count only when the filer's broadest family (a) includes them; they are never read on their own (the finding's open question, answered).
    - **Look-back order: date first (pre-flight minor).** Candidate dates are tried newest first (the bridge date, then each earlier balance within E-7's look-back), and at each date the families are tried in the order (a), (b), (c). The first date at which any family is filed gives the reading, and a family filed as zero at that date ends the look-back as for E-7. This is the order the pre-flight emulation used to reproduce this plan's figures.
  - **Rate-regulated utilities (pre-flight I7, re-ruled at the re-check).** For a filer whose SIC is one of 4911, 4923, 4924, 4931, 4932 or 4941 (`bundle.edgar.sic`, read with `parseSicCode` from `src/edgar/sic.ts`), the pension and OPEB deficits are NOT deducted. These are exactly the codes the repository's own SIC map (`src/edgar/sic.ts:154-169`) sends to a regulated electric, gas or water utility or a diversified utility. The first ruling's range 4900-4991 is withdrawn, because the same map classes 4922 as midstream pipelines, 4953, 4955 and 4959 as waste management and 4991 as cogeneration and small power producers, none of which recovers costs through rates. A waste hauler would have printed a false ASC 980 sentence. The group-49 fallback (4900, 4950, 4961) does not count either. An info note says the deficits are assumed recoverable through rates (ASC 980), because the offsetting regulatory asset is reported only in dimensional XBRL, which companyfacts does not carry. In the census only DUK (4931) and NEE (4911) qualify.
    - **SIC not established (addendum 2, item 4).** A filer whose SIC is not established is read as not rate-regulated. When, and only when, a pension and OPEB line is actually deducted for such a filer, the info gap `valuation.dcf.debtLikeObligations.sicUnknown` says: the filer's SIC code is not established, so it is read as not rate-regulated, and its deficit is deducted. A run with no SIC and no deducted pension line (the DEMO fixture among them) gains no gap.
    - Loss contingencies and contingent consideration are still deducted at a utility, and its E-7 lines take the after-tax rule like any other filer's.
    - DUK keeps main's pension treatment (its 0.384B family-(b) deficit is disclosed, not deducted).
    - Residual, routed to the free-data-source batch: net the deficit against the pension regulatory asset once dimensional XBRL is available.
  - **Loss contingencies:** `LossContingencyAccrualAtCarryingValue`, else `LossContingencyAccrualCarryingValue{Noncurrent,Current}`. A current-only accrual counts as the whole accrual (litigation accruals are usually all current). Only the amount above any same-date `AccrualForEnvironmentalLossContingencies` is deducted, so an environmental accrual inside the total is never counted twice.
  - **Contingent consideration:** `BusinessCombinationContingentConsiderationLiability`, else `...LiabilityNoncurrent` plus `...LiabilityCurrent`, current-only allowed.
  - None of these joins the market EV (ARO and environmental do not either). The EV basis says so in one sentence.
- **Alternatives rejected.** Disclose only: it leaves a recognised claim inside common equity while the bridge itemises E-7 lines as if complete. Before tax for all five (E-7's convention, this plan's first version): practitioner and academic sources measure a pension deficit net of tax (sources below), so the before-tax claim is overstated by the relief a settlement earns; the main session adopted the better-supported convention (decisions spec 4.3). After-tax for pension alone: two tax conventions in one bridge. The filer's own deferred-tax asset per obligation: not tagged consistently (batch 2b's reason for E-7's before-tax choice). A separate positive "tax relief" bridge line, as the investment tax is a separate negative one: the bridge surfaces (`auditTables.ts:97`, `scenarioPresentation.ts:155-166`) read only a component's sign, so a positive line would print as "Plus non-operating …", and carrying a component kind to the display needs a report-schema field; netting each line keeps one line per claim (Task 14's invariant) with no schema change. Crediting overfunded plans: a US employer reversion pays a 20% excise tax, 50% unless a replacement plan or benefit increase applies (IRC 4980(a), (d); https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/4980), on top of income tax, so a surplus is not equity value one-for-one; practitioners ignore it (FE Training below). Adding pension to the market EV: the own-history EV ranks would then compare two definitions until every quarter's pension balance is read; left for later.
- **Revises.** E-7 (D-104, owner decision): extends its scope from two tag families to five, and revises two of its conventions, each said so: (1) the tax convention, before tax → after tax at the D-98 marginal rate, for all five families (decisions spec 4.3; resolves known issue B2b-1); (2) E-7's "a current portion alone is not the obligation" stays for retirement, environmental and pension lines, while a current-only loss-contingency accrual or contingent-consideration liability counts as the whole (litigation and earn-out balances are commonly all current, and a current part alone can only understate the claim, never double it). Revises `docs/audit/2026-10-17-book-to-code-disposition.md:70, :195` (pensions listed as future liquidity work only). `docs/audit/2026-10-22-audit-batch2b-valuation-mediums.md:55` is history and is not edited; the D-next entry says it is superseded.
- **Sources.**
  - ASU 2017-07, as applied in Hexion's FY2017 10-K (https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000013239/000001323918000007/hexion201710k.htm): only the service cost stays in operating income, and the other components of net benefit cost are "presented separately from the service cost component and outside of income from operations". The deficit's financing cost is therefore not in the EBIT the DCF projects.
  - ASC 715 funded-status presentation, summarised at https://www.universalcpareview.com/?p=1380: overfunded plans are aggregated as an asset and underfunded plans as a liability. That is the recognised liability read here.
  - FE Training, "Pensions and OPEBs" (https://felix.fe.training/video-lesson/lesson/pensions-and-opebs/?video=2038), checked 2026-10-27: "you start with an enterprise value, subtract net debt and subtract the pension or OPEB deficit net of any tax liability"; on surpluses, "most analysts kind of ignore overfunded pension schemes". The deficit is a bridge claim net of tax, and a surplus is not credited.
  - Feldstein, "Private Pensions as Corporate Debt", in *The Changing Roles of Debt and Equity in Financing U.S. Capital Formation* (NBER, 1982), pp. 75-90 (https://www.nber.org/chapters/c11396.pdf; bibliographic data at https://ideas.repec.org/h/nbr/nberch/11396.html). The PDF is a scan with no text layer. Its page images were rendered offline and read on 2026-10-28 (decisions spec 4.5, O-4), and were lost in the deletion of the same day (pre-flight I9). The quotes below are as that reading recorded them and were not re-checked; the docs and DECISIONS cite the chapter by its citation and say it was not re-checked (Task 20), and never quote it from memory. The decision does not rest on him: FE Training and IB Interview Questions carry the convention. Page 77: "There is a fundamental difference in the tax treatment of bonds and pension obligations that has an important quantitative effect on the valuation of pension obligations although it does not imply a qualitative difference between bonds and pensions: all the payments made to a pension fund are tax deductible while the principal repayments on a bond are not tax deductible." Pages 77-78: "It is wrong therefore to regard pension liabilities as equivalent to bonds or loan balances. … Equivalently, it is important to measure pension obligations in terms of their net-of-tax cost." Page 78, his example at a 50% rate "including both federal and state taxes" (p. 77): a firm that funds a $100 benefit due in ten years "need contribute only $32.20", and "since the current contribution would be tax deductible, the net cost to the firm would be only $16.10", that is, the pretax-discounted claim times one minus the rate. The same page notes that the present value "depends on the extent to which (or the speed with which) those benefit obligations are funded" (the funding-schedule residual above). The two page images (`feldstein-1982-p77.png`, `-p78.png`) were lost with `tmp/audit-2026-10-27/plans/B4-post-int/`.
  - 26 U.S.C. §461(h) (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/461), checked 2026-10-28: the all events test is not met "any earlier than when economic performance" occurs, and for a liability arising "out of any tort", "economic performance occurs as the payments to such person are made" (§461(h)(2)(C)). This is why a litigation accrual earns its relief when it is paid, which is the timing the closing note assumes.
  - Porte Brown, "Tax issues when buying a business with an earnout" (https://portebrown.com/newsblog-archive/tax-issues-when-buying-a-business-with-an-earnout), checked 2026-10-28, practitioner summary of the buyer's side: the buyer receives "no tax basis from contingent payment amounts until they become fixed", a payment treated as principal "generally creates additional tax basis in the acquired assets or stock", and in an asset purchase the added basis is "often allocated to intangible assets that must be amortized over 15 years". This is the earn-out case the closing note names: the relief comes late (asset deal) or not at all while the shares are held (stock deal).
  - IB Interview Questions, "Pensions and off-balance-sheet hidden liabilities" (https://ibinterviewquestions.com/guides/valuation-investment-banking/pensions-off-balance-sheet-hidden-liabilities), checked 2026-10-28, practitioner convention: "Some analysts apply a tax adjustment because pension contributions are tax-deductible", Pension Adjustment = Unfunded Pension × (1 − Tax Rate); "Overfunded: Plan assets exceed the obligation. No adjustment needed."
  - 26 U.S.C. §404(a) (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/404), checked 2026-10-28: employer contributions "paid by an employer to or under a stock bonus, pension, profit-sharing, or annuity plan" are deductible under §404, subject to its limits, which is why the relief exists. 26 U.S.C. §162(f)(1) (https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/26/162): "no deduction otherwise allowable shall be allowed under this chapter for any amount paid or incurred" to or at the direction of a government in relation to the violation of a law, which is the disclosed exception for some loss contingencies.
  - D-98 "Tax on investments" (`docs/audit/DECISIONS.md:2087`): "The rate is the marginal-rate floor (D-58; 21% in the US, 15% where no statutory rate is sourced)". This is the rate the obligations reuse.
  - Hectelion glossary, "Debt-like items" (https://www.hectelion.com/en-us/glossary/debt-like-items), checked 2026-10-27: "liabilities or commitments that, without being financial debt in the strict sense, are treated as such in the bridge from enterprise value to the equity price"; its examples include "pension provisions" and "earn-outs due". This is the practitioner support for contingent consideration.
  - FE Training, "Debt Equivalents" (https://www.fe.training/free-finance-resources/valuation/debt-equivalents), checked 2026-10-27: lists "pension deficits" among the debt equivalents shown in the enterprise-to-equity bridge. (It does not mention earn-outs; the Hectelion entry above carries that.)
  - Law Insider, "Debt-Like Items" definition (https://www.lawinsider.com/dictionary/debt-like-items): sample purchase-agreement wording defines debt-like items as including "unfunded pension liabilities" and "Specific Litigation Provisions". This is a contractual precedent for recognised litigation provisions; ordinary-course reserves remain a judgment (E-7's disclosure covers it).

### OD-5. Equity-method stakes: value once, income and stake on the same side (FIN-401, FIN-401-V1; B4b)

- **Question.** Take the equity-method income out of EBIT and keep the add-back, or keep the stake in operating capital and drop the add-back? How is partial inclusion treated (segment OI that excludes some investees)?
- **Decision.** The brief's default, with a fallback where it cannot be applied.
  - The statements builder records, per income row, where the equity-method income sits (`equityMethodIncomePlacement`). The test is arithmetic or the derivation's own provenance, never the element name.
  - **Inside, amount filed** (a filed `OperatingIncomeLoss` that the pretax arithmetic shows holds it; a derived EBIT that did not subtract a filed `IncomeLossFromEquityMethodInvestments`): the DCF's EBIT seed and margin history subtract the filed income, and the bridge keeps the stake as a non-operating add-back.
  - **Inside, amount not filed** (a derived EBIT whose filer stopped tagging the income): the stake's equity-method part stays in operating capital and is not added back. Its income and capital are then on the same side.
  - **Partial inclusion** (a filed OI whose own text excludes some investees, which the arithmetic cannot prove either way) is "undetermined". Undetermined keeps today's treatment: below operating income, the default presentation of SEC Regulation S-X Rule 5-03. The note says it is assumed, not shown.
  - **An aggregate cannot show "inside" (pre-flight C1).** Many filers tag their "Total other income and expenses" as `NonoperatingIncomeExpense`, and that total then holds the equity-method income. When the below-OI figure is that aggregate, `rest = pretax − OI − below + interest ≈ 0` cannot show where the income sits, so the period is "undetermined" and main's treatment is kept for that stake. Only the component path (`OtherNonoperatingIncomeExpense` + `InvestmentIncomeInterest`, no aggregate filed) can show "in-operating-income"; Boeing's case is the component path. Measured with the plan's own formula and tolerance (`placement.txt`): CMCSA and DUK read "in-operating-income" in every period under the first rule (rest 0 to 1M; E −591M to +285M and 7M to 51M), and NEE likewise (no interest tag, so the aggregate holds interest and E); DUK publishes a DCF ($36.64), so the misfire would have lowered a published figure by about 1-4%. KO reads "below" correctly (it tags `OtherNonoperatingIncomeExpense` and no aggregate); T, DIS and AMZN read "undetermined"; BA reads "in-operating-income" correctly.
  - **The base window (pre-flight I4).** A filed equity-method income of 0 (or within the placement tolerance of 0) counts as a filed quarter: it is measured at its filed amount and takes the placement the window's other quarters show (BA files 0 for Q1 2026). A derived fourth quarter (fiscal year less nine months) takes the placement computed from its own derived operands, each derived on one presentation by B13's `oneBasisPair` (`src/edgar/presentationLineage.ts:180` at B13), as B13 derives the quarter's other lines; when any operand's pair is withheld, that quarter has no placement and the window keeps main's treatment.
  - **One floor on both routes (pre-flight I5).** The long-term-investments floor below applies on the keyless route and on R0's provider-path stakes fill alike, through one shared chain: `admitVendorLongTermInvestments` (`src/edgar/financialEnrichment.ts:854` at B13; `:754-790` at `main`), which today copies the D-98 chain with `firstInstant(tagsFor("longTermInvestments"))`, calls the builder's `longTermInvestments` chain with its `unlessBelow` floor instead of a copy. No census issuer is affected: no `LongTermInvestments` is below `EquityMethodInvestments` since 2021, except XOM's predecessor 10-K (271M against 32.653B), which feeds only the predecessor-built FY2025 row.
  - The bridge note is worded per case and never claims "below operating income" when the run showed otherwise.
  - A filed `LongTermInvestments` total smaller than the same-date `EquityMethodInvestments` is not the total of the stakes. The LTI chain falls to the piece sum, so 10-K and 10-Q rows of one issuer carry one composition, on both routes (I5 above). The mechanism is B13's component floor (`first.unlessBelow`, B13 OD-4 and Task 1), declared on the `longTermInvestments` synonym entry: us-gaap defines `LongTermInvestments` as the sum of the carrying amounts of all investments intended to be held for an extended period, which includes equity-method investments, so the floor meets B13's rule that a floor is declared only where the definition puts the floor element inside the line item.
  - FMP-route rows carry no derivation provenance and no placement field, so they read as "undetermined" (today's treatment, the note says "assumed"). That is a recorded residual (Task 19), not a silent pass.
  - Relation to the brief's default ("take equity-method income out of the projected EBIT and keep the stake as a non-operating add-back"): applied wherever the income is measured. Where the filer stopped tagging it (ExxonMobil), no figure exists to subtract, and keeping the stake in operating capital is the only treatment that counts it once without inventing an amount; that is the one deviation.
- **Alternatives rejected.** Always keep the stake in operating capital: it throws away a filed income figure and changes every equity-method issuer (KO, T, utilities) whose OI correctly excludes it. Always subtract the income: it double-removes it for the majority, whose income already sits below OI under Rule 5-03, an error of several margin points for large-affiliate issuers.
- **Revises.** D-60 (premise "their income is below the EBIT this DCF projects", `valuation.ts:2145-2148`), D-65 item 4 (the bridge adds back exactly what sales-to-capital removed; still true, now per case), D-98 "Investment stakes" (the LTI chain's total-first order gains the same-date floor), D-55 (equity-method income carried as filed, now consumed).
- **Sources.**
  - SEC Regulation S-X Rule 5-03 (https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/210.5-03), checked 2026-10-27: captions 10 "Income or loss before income tax expense and appropriate items below", 11 "Income tax expense", 12 "Equity in earnings of unconsolidated subsidiaries and 50 percent or less owned persons", with the proviso "If justified by the circumstances, this item may be presented in a different position and a different manner." The default puts the income below pretax, so "below OI" is the right default, and the proviso is why a filer that departs from it (Boeing, ExxonMobil) must be detected, not presumed.
  - Damodaran, "Holdings in Other Firms" (https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~adamodar/New_Home_Page/valquestions/valcrosshold.htm), checked 2026-10-27: "You would value the equity in each holding separately and estimate the value of the proportional holding", and, where the income is consolidated, it must be stripped first: "If you do not do so, you will double count the value of the subsidiary." A holding is counted through its income or through its stake, not both. (The plan's earlier citation, `pdfiles/eqexams/ReviewCrossHoldings.pdf`, is a compressed PDF that could not be read for verification; use this page.)
  - Trainer, "Unconsolidated Subsidiary Assets – Valuation Adjustment" (https://www.wealthmanagement.com/investment-news/unconsolidated-subsidiary-assets-valuation-adjustment): when the subsidiary income is inside NOPAT the assets are in invested capital; when it is not, the assets are added to shareholder value. Income and capital go on the same side.

### OD-6. Long-term investments that exceed invested capital (FIN-304)

- **Question.** At the boundary, keep the D-58 fallback (sales-to-capital on full capital and no add-back) or move to a continuous rule? Is the holding added back either way?
- **Decision.** Always add back. A filed non-operating holding is added in the bridge at carrying value less the D-98 tax whether or not removing it leaves positive operating capital.
  - When removing it leaves no positive operating capital, the operating ratio is unbounded, and sales-to-capital takes the house cap `S2C_CLAMP[1]` (5.0), the limit the ratio approaches from below. The clamp note names the reason.
  - The A-2 history counts such a year at the cap too.
  - DCF equity value then never falls when investments rise.
- **Alternatives rejected.** Keep the D-58 fallback: the holding is valued at zero (FCFF excludes investment income), and the value drops discontinuously, up to 4.8% a share for 1B more investments in the audit's case B. Withhold the DCF at the boundary: it removes a figure for a balance-sheet shape the model can value.
- **Revises.** The D-58 fallback documented at `docs/METHODOLOGY.md:2251` and the D-60 code note "When removing them would leave no operating capital the D-58 basis stands" (`valuation.ts:2148-2149`). D-65 item 4 stays true: the bridge adds back what sales-to-capital removed, and now also what it could not remove.
- **Sources.**
  - D-65's own principle (`docs/METHODOLOGY.md:1166-1169`): "an asset left out of operating capital is never left out of value too". The converse is the same accounting identity.
  - Damodaran, "Holdings in Other Firms" (URL in OD-5): each holding is valued separately and added to the operating value at its own value. No method values a filed holding at zero because the operating capital is small.

### OD-7. The divisor in a loss period (FIN-404)

- **Question.** In a loss quarter, whose filed diluted count equals basic, which count divides the value? The options are the newest profitable-period diluted count, basic plus the filed overhang, or disclose only.
- **Decision.** The loss row's basic count plus the newest award-dilution increment from a profitable period among the latest four quarters and the latest fiscal year. The increment is that period's diluted count less its basic count, less any filed conversion increment for that period (OD-2 handles conversion shares).
  - The substitution is dated and disclosed (an info gap `valuation.perShare.lossPeriodDivisor`).
  - The same divisor reaches the DCF, excess-return and REIT per share, and forecast EPS.
  - With no profitable donor period, the basic count stands, and the D-60 note and METHODOLOGY say it carries no option or RSU dilution.
  - Detection: the divisor row's diluted count is at or below its basic count, and its net income (continuing operations when filed) is negative.
- **Alternatives rejected.** The newest profitable-period diluted count: it carries that period's older basic count, undoing buybacks or issuance since. Disclose only: every per-share figure reads high by the overhang every year for seasonal issuers.
- **Revises.** D-65 item 5 and the D-60 divisor disclosure ("the diluted count is kept so the per-share value carries the dilution the market cap omits"); METHODOLOGY "Share basis" (`docs/METHODOLOGY.md:1181`).
- **Sources.**
  - Deloitte Roadmap, *Earnings per Share* 8.7 (https://dart.deloitte.com/USDART/home/codification/presentation/asc260-10/roadmap-earnings-per-share/chapter-8-other-considerations/8-7-discontinued-operations): the control number is income from continuing operations, and "if there is a loss from continuing operations, diluted EPS would be computed in the same manner as basic EPS". The filed diluted count in a loss period therefore omits awards that still exist.
  - ASC 260-10-45-17 to 45-20 (antidilution), quoted in the same roadmap. The awards are excluded for the EPS figure only, not extinguished.

### OD-8. One REIT market cap and EV; redeemable OP units (FIN-119-V2 OP-unit question, FIN-311)

- **Question.** Are redeemable OP units added to the REIT EV when the diluted count may already hold them? (The same question applies to the permanent-NCI term.) Which market cap prices the REIT block?
- **Decision.** The REIT block takes its market cap and its EV from the multiples framework: the quote's admitted market cap and the house EV, which after OD-1 holds preferred, redeemable preferred, book NCI and redeemable NCI.
  - The quote's market cap counts common shares outstanding only. OP units, permanent or redeemable, are not common shares, so adding them as NCI never double counts.
  - P/FFO and P/AFFO become market cap / FFO at both sites. FFO and AFFO per share stay on the diluted count. One P/FFO, one EV and one cap rate per report, so the existing note and METHODOLOGY:1955-1956 become literally true.
- **Alternatives rejected.** Price x weighted diluted shares: it gives two P/FFOs and two EVs per report (1.2% apart for Realty Income) and can count OP units twice once redeemable NCI joins the EV. Moving the multiples to price / FFO per diluted share: equally valid, but it touches peer and own-history P/FFO, which are not in this batch.
- **Revises.** D-26's sentence "the REIT implied cap rate uses the house enterprise value" (`DECISIONS.md:1819`): made true by construction instead of by a parallel formula.
- **Sources.**
  - Deloitte Roadmap 9.4 (URL in OD-1): common-share and preferred-share redeemable NCI is carried in temporary equity and adjusted to its redemption amount, so its carrying amount is already the claim it adds to the EV. The roadmap does not discuss OP units (checked 2026-10-27, whole page). That an UPREIT's OP units redeemable for cash at the holder's option are such an NCI is filer practice: REIT 10-Ks carry "noncontrolling interests ... reported as temporary equity" at "the greater of the carrying amount and the redemption value", while units the REIT may settle in its own shares stay in permanent equity as book NCI. Both reach the EV through the NCI terms, never through the common share count.
  - `docs/METHODOLOGY.md:1955-1956` (the repository's own definition: "the same definition the multiples and the DCF bridge use, so a report carries one EV").

### OD-9. Fair-value financial assets outside cash and short-term investments (VAL-14; B4b, pre-flight addendum)

- **Question.** The DCF equity bridge leaves out Amazon's Anthropic convertible notes: available-for-sale debt securities carried at fair value, 97.9B at 2026-06-30 (45.8B at 2025-12-31), worth about $7 a share (claim audit of live AMZN report #52, F-04). They sit in "Other assets", and their only tag is the undivided `AvailableForSaleSecuritiesDebtSecurities`, so D-98's stakes chain leaves them out. R0 (D-133) kept them out under D-98 as a conservative choice and recorded the question for later; R1's OD-9 recommended keeping them out and routed the question to B5. B4 owns the bridge, so B4b decides it (pre-flight addendum), and R1's routing is superseded (R1 plan, cross-batch).
- **Research first (Task 25 Step 0).** Is a long-term available-for-sale debt security at fair value a non-operating financial asset of the bridge? The executor reads Damodaran's treatment of cash and marketable securities and the equity bridge of R0 (D-133) and D-98, and records the answer with its quotes and access dates before any code. A source that cannot be fetched is marked unverified and is not the decision's basis.
- **Decision (the addendum's rule, applied after Step 0).**
  - The bridge adds the financial assets that are reported at fair value and sit outside cash and short-term investments, exactly once: never a second time beside `longTermInvestments`, the D-98 stakes fill or Task 8's equity-method stake.
  - Each is its own bridge line, named with its filed tag and amount (`millions()`), and taxed like the other holdings at the D-98 investment-tax rate.
  - An asset that already sits inside cash and short-term investments is never added (the control).
- **Guard (merge gate).** R1's OD-9 measured the undivided tag holding ordinary portfolios at most census filers at their latest dates: NVIDIA 46.9B (against current marketable securities of 49.1B), Microsoft 71.1B, Alphabet 99.5B (against 186.6B), UnitedHealth 50.3B, Progressive 92.4B and Tesla 41.6B. Task 25 Step 0 therefore lists, for every census issuer, what the rule would add and why it is outside cash and short-term investments. If the rule would add an ordinary treasury or insurance portfolio already inside them, or one whose placement the filing does not establish, Task 25 stops and reports to the controller before Step 1.
- **Expected.** AMZN-shaped inputs ≈ +$7 a share; a control that carries the securities inside cash and short-term investments: no change. B4b's replay re-measures AMZN #51 and #52 (Task 21b).
- **Revises.** D-98's "leaving the undivided available-for-sale total out", for the assets the rule establishes as outside cash and short-term investments; D-133's VAL-14 residual; R1 OD-9's routing to B5.
- **Sources.** Recorded in Task 25 Step 0. Starting points: Damodaran, "Holdings in Other Firms" (URL in OD-5); ASC 320-10-50-2 (by major security type, via Deloitte; R1 OD-9's source list); Regulation S-X Rule 5-02, captions 2 and 12 (marketable securities separate from "Other investments").

### Recorded rules revised without an owner decision

- FIN-403 applies E-7 and B-36 (D-112) to a successor registrant's predecessor facts. It revises the in-code rule "Only the issuer's own XBRL facts count" (`debtLikeObligations.ts:8`) for one case: a successor with no annual report of its own whose continuity with the predecessor B-36 has established.
- FIN-103 makes D-65 item 5 ("The bridge stays at the statement-date balance") true when the DCF base falls back to the annual statement while a newer quarterly balance exists: the bridge reads the statement-date balance.

## Review Focus

Input classes that no probe exercises; each line gets a test in the owning task. Items 1-7 are the plan's own; items 8-15 are the risks the pre-flight review found (I12) and the addendum. The part each test ships in is named.

1. **(B4a) Redeemable NCI beside an E-5 earnings-share key above book.** The NCI must be deducted once, at the larger of (book + redeemable NCI) and profit share x consolidated equity value. Task 2 test "redeemable NCI is the E-5 floor, not a second deduction".
2. **(B4b) A conversion increment filed only for a year-to-date duration (six or nine months), not the divisor's quarter.** It must not be read as the quarter's N. Task 11 test "a year-to-date increment is not the quarter's".
3. **(B4a) Two pension tag families filed at one date** (`PensionAndOtherPostretirementDefinedBenefitPlansLiabilitiesNoncurrent` and `DefinedBenefitPensionPlanLiabilitiesNoncurrent`). The claim must be counted once, from the broadest family. Task 6 test "two pension families at one date count once".
4. **(B4a) Operating-lease scope proven on the bridge row but unproven on an A-2 history year.** One median is on one lease basis (pre-flight minor): the unproven year cannot be put on the bridge row's lease-excluded basis, so it is left out of the median with a note, never kept lease-inclusive beside lease-excluded years. Task 4 test "an unproven history year is left out of a lease-excluded median".
5. **(B4a) A lessee whose lease-excluded invested capital, less long-term investments, turns non-positive** (FIN-303 x FIN-304). Sales-to-capital takes the cap, the holding is added back, and equity value is monotone in the holding. Task 5 test "lease exclusion that empties operating capital takes the cap". It covers capital less long-term investments ≤ 0; total capital ≤ 0 is item 13.
6. **(B4a) A settlement fund that partly offsets a retirement obligation, under the after-tax convention** (integration addition). The tax relief applies to the amount deducted (obligation less the offset), never to the offset, and a fully offset obligation deducts nothing and earns no relief. Task 6 re-pins "credits only the part of a settlement fund …" (−200 → −170 at 15%, and its note names both amounts) and keeps "does not say an obligation the fund fully offsets is deducted" unchanged.
7. **(B4a) A successor and its predecessor that both tag one family** (post-integration review; the XOM census shape: pension 9.068B in the successor's 10-Q at 2026-06-30, 8.940B in the predecessor's at 2026-03-31). The family is read once, from the successor; only families the successor does not tag come from the predecessor. Task 7 test "a family both registrants tag is read once, from the successor".
8. **(B4b; C1) A non-operating aggregate that holds the equity-method income** (the CMCSA, DUK and NEE shapes: `NonoperatingIncomeExpense` filed, `rest ≈ 0`). The placement must read "undetermined" and the bridge must be main's. Task 8 test "an aggregate that holds the equity-method income cannot show it is inside", with CMCSA and NEE shapes as controls.
9. **(B4b; C2) A convertible preferred the bridge did not deduct, issued in the period** (the GOOGL shape: `preferredStock` null, convertible-preferred value 18.023B, 0 at the prior quarter, a quarter's increment of 16M). The claim is deducted once, and the converted reading restores no more than that. Task 11 tests "a convertible preferred issued in the period is deducted once, and its converted reading restores no more" and "a convertible with no filed carrying value keeps main's figure and names the instrument".
10. **(B4a; I2) A null book NCI beside a redeemable NCI.** Never 0 plus the redeemable NCI: the per share stays withheld under E-5, with the redeemable NCI named. Task 2 test "a null book NCI beside a redeemable NCI stays withheld (E-5)".
11. **(B4b; I4) A filed zero quarter and a derived fourth quarter in the base window** (the BA shape: Q1 2026 filed 0; Q4 2025 derived). The window is still measured, and the derived quarter's placement comes from operands paired by `oneBasisPair`. Task 8 test "a filed zero quarter and a derived fourth quarter keep the window measured".
12. **(B4b; I5) The long-term-investments floor on the provider route.** R0's stakes fill must take the builder's floor, not a copy. Task 8 test "the provider stakes fill takes the builder's floor".
13. **(B4a; I6) Lease-emptied total capital** (a negative-equity lessee once the lease leaves debt). Task 4 tests "lease-emptied capital takes the issuer's own history on the same basis" and "lease-emptied capital without three usable years withholds the DCF and says why".
14. **(B4a; I7) A rate-regulated utility's pension deficit.** Disclosed, not deducted; its loss contingencies and earn-outs still deducted. Task 6 test "a rate-regulated utility's pension deficit is disclosed, not deducted", with a non-utility control.
15. **(B4b; addendum) Fair-value financial assets outside cash and short-term investments** (the AMZN Anthropic notes). Added once, never beside the same asset in long-term investments, the stakes fill or the equity-method stake. Task 25 tests "AMZN-shaped notes are added once and named" and "securities inside cash and short-term investments add nothing".

## Part A: B4a (PR 1)

Branch `fix/equity-bridge-and-invested-capital`, cut after B13 merges. Tasks run in this order: 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 9, 12, 13, then B4a's batch tasks 14-24. Order matters: Task 1 creates the one bridge row every later bridge fix reads; Tasks 2-3 add the senior-claims predicate that Task 11 (B4b) extends with the convertible-preferred claim; Task 4's lease predicate precedes Task 5's capital floor. Tasks 8, 10 and 11 are in Part B. Every test file is new unless named as a re-pin.

### Task 1: The DCF bridge reads the statement-date balance (FIN-103)

**Files:**
- Modify `src/pipeline/compute.ts:3261-3263` (beside `pickBalanceAnchor`, add the bridge row), `:3368-3384` (`dcfBalance` from the bridge row; `dcfCapitalHistory` `:3384`), `:3646-3673` (extract the `multiplesBalance` literal into `toMultiplesBalance(row, basis)` so the bridge row reuses it; no field changes; it already carries `fieldConflicts`, R0), `:3915-3970` (`netDebt`, `minorityInterest`, `preferred`, `noncontrollingOwnershipUnresolved`, `leaseLiability`, `operatingLeaseLiability`, `nonOperatingAssets`, `longTermInvestmentsBasis`, `debtLikeObligations` read the bridge row; `multiples.balance`, `reit` and `excessReturn` keep `balPoint`).
- Modify `src/pipeline/stageB/valuation.ts:5650-5715` (`ValuationBundleInputs.bridgeBalance`), `:5807` and `:6146-6182` (the DCF-only NCI gate and lease checks read `inputs.bridgeBalance ?? inputs.multiples.balance`), `:2208-2220` (the valuation-date basis names the balance actually bridged).
- Test: `tests/stageB.bridgeBalance.audit20261027.test.ts`.

**Interfaces:**
- Produces `export function dcfBridgeRow<T extends { date?: unknown } & Partial<Record<"totalDebt" | "totalStockholdersEquity" | "cashAndShortTermInvestments", unknown>>>(statementDate: string | null, quarterly: readonly T[], annual: readonly T[], fallback: { row: T | null; basis: "quarter" | "annual" | null }): { row: T | null; basis: "quarter" | "annual" | null; matchesStatement: boolean }` (compute.ts, exported like `pickBalanceAnchor`).
- Produces `ValuationBundleInputs.bridgeBalance?: MultiplesBalance | null`.
- Produces manifest field `valuation.dcf.bridgeBalanceDate` (info) when no balance row is dated at the statement date.
- Consumes `DcfIncomeRow.date` / `.basis` (the statement date), `pickBalanceAnchor` (fallback).

  - `buildDcfAssumptions` compares `inputs.balance.date` with `statementDate`. When the balance is LATER, the roll-forward basis says "the equity bridge uses the {balance} balance, {n} days after the statement date; flows between the two dates are in both that balance and the first window". When it is EARLIER, it says "the equity bridge uses the {balance} balance, {n} days before the statement date; flows between the two dates are in neither that balance nor the first window". Either way it pushes the info gap. When equal, the D-65 sentence stands. The same comparison runs when the quote is beyond the roll-forward limit (the "stated at the statement date" note names the bridge balance too).

**Interfaces:**
- Produces:
  ```ts
  export interface TemporaryEquityRow { temporaryEquity?: number | null; temporaryEquityAttributableToParent?: number | null;
    temporaryEquityAttributableToNoncontrollingInterest?: number | null; fieldConflicts?: Record<string, { usable?: boolean; reason?: string } | undefined> }
  export interface TemporaryEquityClaims { redeemablePreferred: number; redeemableNoncontrolling: number; unsplit: number; total: number; basis: string; refusal: string | null }
  export function temporaryEquityClaims(row: TemporaryEquityRow | null | undefined): TemporaryEquityClaims | null; // null when nothing is filed
  ```
  The total-vs-parts tolerance is Stage A's `IDENTITY_TOLERANCE_PCT`, imported, not copied.
- Produces `ValuationBundleInputs.temporaryEquity?: TemporaryEquityClaims | null`, and `DcfBridge.temporaryEquity?: { redeemablePreferred: number; redeemableNoncontrolling: number; unsplit: number; basis: string }` (disclosure only; the amounts are already inside `bridge.preferred` / `bridge.minorityInterest`).
- Produces manifest fields `valuation.dcf.temporaryEquityAllocation` (warn) and `valuation.multiples.enterpriseValue.temporaryEquity` (warn).
- **A withheld enrichment is unknown, not absent (addendum 2, item 7; M11).** Produces `ValuationBundleInputs.temporaryEquityUnknown?: { date: string; reason: string } | null` and manifest field `valuation.temporaryEquity.unknown` (warn).
  - compute.ts sets it when the bridge row has no observed temporary-equity field (all three null) and `bundle.gaps` holds a warn entry whose `field` is `statements.balance.{bridgeDate}.temporaryEquity` or `statements.balance.{bridgeDate}.{one of the three fields}` and whose `reason` starts `"temporary-equity SEC enrichment withheld"`, EXCEPT the SEC-silent refusal (second re-check, finding 1). The matched paths of `supplementTemporaryEquity` (`src/edgar/temporaryEquityEnrichment.ts`) are:
    - the `refuse` closure (`:29-32`) at its call sites `:33` (vendor CIK unknown or different), `:35` (no compatible SEC period and currency) and `:42` (vendor permanent-equity scope unknown);
    - the per-category gap at `:66` (SEC holds a value whose operands could not be matched).
  - **Excluded: `:81`**, whose reason ends `temporary carrying amounts are absent or explicitly refused for this SEC period`. It means the SEC period itself holds no temporary equity. It fires on every row of an issuer that tagged temporary equity in any period (stored MU #46 carries it at `2026-05-28` and `2026-09-03`, while MU filed temporary equity only in 2017-2018), so reading it would put a false "may be missing" gap on every such issuer.
  - **Not read: the vendor-CIK-mismatch gap at `src/edgar/financialEnrichment.ts:903`** (`field: statements.{kind}.{date}`, reason `SEC financial/temporary-equity enrichment withheld: vendor issuer differs from confirmed company facts`). It fires for every issuer, whether or not it tags temporary equity, so it cannot show that a claim exists. This is a disclosed residual (Task 19).
  - compute.ts reads the merged `bundle.gaps`, which holds one entry per `field` (`mergeManifest`, `src/pipeline/stageA/manifest.ts:54-75`). At a date where the annual and quarterly enrichments differ, the surviving entry decides.
  - A vendor/SEC conflict (`:72`) is not this case: it sets `fieldConflicts` and is Task 2's refusal.
  - The DCF bridge, the market EV, the own-history EV and the key-data EV keep main's figures (no claim is added, and nothing is withheld). The one warn gap says: "At {date} the issuer tags temporary equity, but its SEC enrichment was withheld ({reason}); a redeemable preferred or redeemable noncontrolling claim could not be established and may be missing from the DCF bridge and the enterprise values, which keep their figures without it."
  - Task 3 likewise keeps main's ROIC and sales-to-capital for that date, and adds one ROIC note.
- Consumes `noncontrollingDeduction` (unchanged), Stage A `fieldConflicts`, `bundle.gaps`.
